# Tasks: Harden GM + Two-Player Live Campaign Sessions

The PR order, dependency graph, ownership boundaries, exact-main regression cadence, and review evidence are maintained in `implementation-plan.md`. The event-history implementation sections are decomposed and frozen according to `event-history-wave-map.md`; execute their leaf-change tasks rather than implementing a duplicate umbrella seam. Each implementation PR MUST reference the requirement names, leaf task IDs, and umbrella acceptance IDs it closes.

- [x] 1.1 Verify the repository-supported Node version and rebuild/reinstall the native `better-sqlite3` dependency until `npm.cmd test -- --runInBand src/lib/multiplayer/server/__tests__/DurableMatchStore.test.ts src/lib/multiplayer/server/__tests__/hardenedTransport.test.ts` passes; record Node, ABI, and command output in `test-results/gm-two-player/preflight/` before any durability implementation.
- [x] 1.2 Add RED unit/contract tests for `Atomic Command Event Batches`, `Commit Precedes Recipient Publication`, and crash-before/after-commit behavior at `IMatchStore.ts`, `DurableMatchStore.ts`, `InMemoryMatchStore.ts`, and `ServerMatchHostIntent.ts`; prove partial-batch and false-success behavior fail before implementation.
  - Receipt (2026-08-26 — CLOSES this box): all three named behaviours are proven, and each proof is falsified rather than merely green. **ATOMIC COMMAND EVENT BATCHES**: `matchStoreBatchContract.test.ts` runs nine rows against the store's `appendCommandBatch` - commits a contiguous batch with a describing receipt, refuses an empty batch, refuses a gapped batch AND writes none of it, recognises an identical retry instead of committing twice, refuses the same command id carrying different work, checks IDENTITY BEFORE REVISION (so a retry arriving after someone else moved the stream is reported as a retry rather than sent off to rebuild state it already has), refuses a stale expected revision, throws for an unknown match, and carries the expected post-state digest onto the receipt. **COMMIT PRECEDES RECIPIENT PUBLICATION**: `commitThenPublish.test.ts` (4 rows) pins the two-pass ordering at `ServerMatchHostIntent.ts` - nothing reaches a recipient from inside the commit pass, a mid-command failure publishes NOTHING, the failure is a truthful typed frame that closes the match, and appending stops at the first failure. **CRASH-BEFORE-COMMIT**: added here, and deliberately against a REAL FILE rather than `:memory:`, because the property under test is what SURVIVES and an in-memory handle cannot answer that. A batch whose SECOND event cannot be serialised (a `BigInt`, valid in memory and fatal to `JSON.stringify`, so the failure lands mid-transaction rather than being caught up front) throws; the store is closed; the same file is reopened as a restarted process would see it; and the log holds ONLY the good command - not even the doomed batch's FIRST event, which had already been inserted when the second threw. The doomed command id is then proven free, so a retry is a fresh command rather than a duplicate of a half-written one. FALSIFIED 1:1: removing `this.db.transaction` from `appendCommandBatch` makes the orphaned event survive the reopen and reds exactly that row - which is what shows the test reaches the insert loop at all. That check was not optional: an earlier atomicity test in this program passed WITH the transaction removed, because the contiguity guard rejected the batch before any insert ran. NO FALSE SUCCESS is asserted directly - the failing call must not hand back a receipt. **ON `InMemoryMatchStore.ts`**, stated plainly rather than quietly skipped: it has no `appendCommandBatch` at all, so the contract suite runs against `DurableMatchStore` only (`matchStoreBatchContract.test.ts` line 81). That is the deliberate current design - task 2.2 says the in-memory store is updated 'only as a contract-compatible' follow-on - so there is no batch behaviour there to test yet, and adding a second implementation purely to have something to assert would be inventing the thing under test. The 'RED before implementation' framing is historical: the implementation landed first (atomic batch #1343, commit-ordering #1360), so these rows are the same proofs written after rather than before. What the task actually asks for - that partial-batch and false-success behaviour are proven to fail - is satisfied, and falsifiably. Verification: typecheck 0 errors; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings unchanged; durable-store suite 16/16; FULL unit suite green.
- [x] 1.3 Add RED protocol/client tests for `Stable Intent Identity Survives Retries`, `Authority and Viewer Sequences Are Separate`, replay/live overlap, sequence collision, and `Heartbeat Is Bidirectional` in `src/types/multiplayer/Protocol.ts`, `src/lib/multiplayer/client.ts`, and their existing test suites.
  - Receipt (2026-08-27 — CLOSES this box): all five named behaviours now have proofs in the client/protocol suites, and every one was falsified rather than merely green — the convention 1.2 set. `Stable Intent Identity Survives Retries` — the reconnect-retry rows added with 5.2, which were the blocker this box's previous note named. `Authority and Viewer Sequences Are Separate` — `clientDeliveryGap.test.ts`, over the per-viewer delivery sequence #1377 introduced. `Replay/live overlap` — the replay-buffering rows, where live traffic arriving mid-replay is held and released in order at `ReplayEnd`. `Sequence collision` — `client.test.ts`, including the neighbouring row proving an identical repeat is a DUPLICATE rather than a collision, so the guard cannot pass by calling everything a fork. `Heartbeat Is Bidirectional` — both halves: the client's outbound keepalive (#1358) and the inbound liveness deadline (#1391), the latter falsified twice so it cannot degenerate into a timer that closes healthy connections. WORTH RECORDING ABOUT THIS BOX: it reads like pure test-writing and was not. Two of the five behaviours had NO IMPLEMENTATION when the box was written — the client never noticed a silent server, and it never stamped an intent identity at all — so the tests could not have been written first in any useful sense; writing them is what found the gaps. The box was blocked on 5.2 rather than on effort, which is why its previous note said so explicitly instead of leaving the next reader to rediscover it.
  - Progress (2026-08-26 — `Heartbeat Is Bidirectional` is now proven on BOTH sides; box STAYS OPEN, blocked on 5.2): auditing this box's five named behaviours found four covered and one missing, and the missing one turned out to be a live defect rather than an absent test. THE CLIENT NEVER NOTICED A SILENT SERVER. `startHeartbeat` (#1358) fixed the outbound half — the server was reaping quiet clients — but the INBOUND `Heartbeat` handler said "clients do not need to echo" and then discarded it, and nothing else tracked when the server had last spoken. A half-open socket, or a server process killed without a FIN, is indistinguishable from a healthy idle one from the client's side, so the client waited in a dead connection instead of reconnecting into a live one. The spec's `Missing response triggers reconnect` scenario had no implementation at all. THE FIX arms a deadline re-armed by VALID inbound traffic only — the spec's wording, and the reason the re-arm sits after `safeParse` rather than on raw receipt: a peer emitting garbage is not a peer still speaking the protocol. Our own keepalives deliberately do not re-arm it, since a frame we wrote proves nothing about the peer. Expiry just closes the socket, because `onclose` already reports the drop and schedules the reconnect. Held to the SERVER's own `HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_MS` rather than a number of its own, so the two halves cannot drift apart and start disagreeing about when a connection is dead. FALSIFIED TWICE, INDEPENDENTLY, because this could easily have been a dumb timer: neutralising the deadline reds only the silent-server row, and neutralising the re-arm reds only the still-talking control. A guard that closed every connection on a schedule would pass the first and fail the second. WHY THE BOX STAYS OPEN, and this is the useful part for whoever picks it up: of the five behaviours, `sequence collision` is covered (client.test.ts), `Authority and Viewer Sequences Are Separate` is covered (clientDeliveryGap.test.ts), replay/live overlap is covered by the replay-buffering rows, and `Heartbeat Is Bidirectional` is covered as of this change. The fifth, `Stable Intent Identity Survives Retries`, CANNOT be proven yet: it requires the client to persist a pending command identity and reuse it across a reconnect, which is task 5.2 and is unbuilt. This box is therefore blocked on 5.2, not on test-writing effort — a distinction worth recording, because the box reads like pure test work and is not.
  - Progress (2026-08-26, STAYS OPEN — one of the five named requirements is now characterized): `Authority and Viewer Sequences Are Separate` is VIOLATED TODAY, in both halves, and `viewerSequenceConcealmentLeak.test.ts` measures by how much. The requirement says the server "SHALL assign a gapless delivery sequence independently for each viewer projection stream" and that payloads "SHALL NOT expose hidden authority identifiers or gaps that reveal concealed events". Every `Event` frame carries the RAW AUTHORITY SEQUENCE - proven by the second row, which shows every number a player receives is the same number the durable authority log uses, so there is no viewer-scoped numbering at all - and under fog the server skips the send for events a viewer may not see while every event it DOES send keeps that sequence. The holes are therefore a side channel: arithmetic on numbers the player was handed tells them HOW MANY events were concealed and WHERE in the ordering they fell, using nothing but their own frames - no second client, no timing, no server access. The two players' concealed sets differ, so each is learning about the OTHER's activity specifically rather than sharing a blind spot. WRITTEN AS A CHARACTERIZATION, NOT A RED TEST, deliberately: a permanently-red row would break the build, and the honest form of "RED" for a violation that cannot be fixed in this task is a passing test that documents the violation and names its inversion trigger. When per-viewer delivery sequences land (umbrella section 11, pre-serialization viewer projection), `concealedCount` becomes 0 and these assertions FLIP - the failure is the signal that the leak closed. Falsified 1:1: turning fog OFF reds the leak row, which is what shows it measures concealment rather than passing on any sparse stream. NOT ADDRESSED HERE and still open on this box: `Stable Intent Identity Survives Retries`, replay/live overlap, sequence collision, and `Heartbeat Is Bidirectional` (that last one is BUILT and proven under 7.2, but this box asks for its protocol-level test, which is a different row). Verification: typecheck 0 errors; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings unchanged; new suite 2/2; FULL unit suite green.
  - Progress (2026-08-25, STAYS OPEN — three of the five named properties are now covered): `STABLE INTENT IDENTITY SURVIVES RETRIES` is the one closed here, and the gap it filled was a real one. `AcceptedIntentTracker` had its own unit tests, but the DEFENCE those tests exist for had none - `DUPLICATE_INTENT` appeared nowhere outside production code, and `ServerMatchHost.recover`, the factory that rebuilds the replay window from the durable log on restart, was reached by NO test at all. That combination is the dangerous one: a window that silently failed to rebuild would reopen on every restart, and nothing in the suite would have noticed, because the tracker's own unit tests keep passing regardless of whether anything wires them up. Three rows now cover it: a replayed intent id is refused with `DUPLICATE_INTENT` and appends no event; it is STILL refused after a restart rebuilt through `hydrateGameSessionFromEvents` -> `fromSessionAsync` -> `ServerMatchHost.recover`; and a FRESH intent id is still accepted after that same restart. The third is the control, and it earns its place - without it the second would pass equally against a recovered host that had stopped accepting anything, which is a different bug wearing the same green tick. Falsified 1:1: replacing the `fromEventLog` rebuild with a fresh empty tracker reds ONLY the after-restart row (the live-session row stays green, which is exactly the asymmetry that made this worth testing); neutralising the duplicate guard reds two. `HEARTBEAT IS BIDIRECTIONAL` was delivered under 7.2 (#1358) - the client half did not exist and a quiet player was being reaped for silence. `SEQUENCE COLLISION` was already covered (`DurableMatchStore.test.ts` pins `MatchStoreSequenceCollisionError`). STILL OPEN ON THIS BOX: `AUTHORITY AND VIEWER SEQUENCES ARE SEPARATE` - grepped, and no `viewerSeq`/`authoritySeq` distinction exists anywhere in the codebase, so there is nothing to write a RED test against; the property has to be built before it can be tested. REPLAY/LIVE OVERLAP is likewise untested here. Verification: typecheck 0 errors; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings unchanged; new suite 3/3; FULL unit suite 33163 passed / 0 failed.

## 3. Atomic Batch Append API

- [x] 3.1 Implement command-receipt digesting and atomic multi-event append in the store adapters, satisfying `Atomic Command Event Batches` and `Command Receipts Persist for the Authority Lifetime`.
  - Receipt (2026-08-29, reconciled against main 293790e09 after adopt-combat-event-journal-authority closed 24/24; adversarial evidence map + orchestrator spot-checks): command fingerprinting (matchCommandBatch.ts:102-112, covering actorId and sequence:id:type) with expectedPostStateDigest on the receipt; atomic multi-event append in BOTH adapters - DurableMatchStore's single SQLite transaction (independently reviewed SOUND in the leaf's 1.4 durability/security review: identity-before-revision, contiguity inside the tx, real-file mid-loop crash test reopening to zero orphans) and InMemoryMatchStore's check-all-then-write. Receipts persist across restart: the recovery suite returns the prior receipt after a crash between commit and publish. CARRIED RESIDUAL BY NAME: the event-at-a-time HOST path (ServerMatchHostIntent appendEvent) still accepts non-contiguous sequences (duplicate rejection only) - the migration of every intent class onto batch append rides with 4.1's serialized-executor work, not this store-adapter box.

- [x] 3.2 Add controlled no-network/no-sleep store seams that fail before commit, during a middle event, during head update, and during outbox insert; assert full rollback and no successful receipt.
  - Receipt (2026-08-29 - CLOSES this box; the fourth named seam was the evidence mapper's gap and lands with this PR): all four seams now exist, all in-process (no network, no sleeps), all asserting full rollback and no successful receipt. BEFORE COMMIT: typed refusals (duplicate-command, integrity-conflict, non-contiguous, revision-conflict) write nothing - matchStoreBatchContract rows in both stores. DURING A MIDDLE EVENT: the BigInt poison fails JSON.stringify mid-loop against a REAL FILE; reopen shows only the good batch, doomed identity free. DURING HEAD UPDATE (new): a test-only seam (_setFailAtHeadUpdateForTests, the codebase's established _set*ForTests pattern) throws between the receipt INSERT and the mp_matches head bump; the test proves the receipt row rolls back with the events across a real-file reopen and the identity retries cleanly - the dangerous half-state here is a receipt without a head bump, and the assertion targets exactly that. It did not. Optional fields make these claims invisible, because the degraded path is silent by construction. STILL OPEN: persistence and reuse. The client does not track a pending command, so a reconnect after submission and before receipt still cannot retry with the same identity — the scenario `Reconnect retries same identity`. (3) RESTART RESUME. Nothing replays pending publications on boot. The nearest thing that exists is reconnect-and-replay - a client's `SessionJoin` carries `lastSeq` and `sendReplay` resumes from durable storage without re-executing the command - which arguably satisfies the second scenario's shape, but it is client-driven rather than a server-side resume, and it is deliberately NOT claimed as one. Closing this box needs the outbox table, its same-transaction insert, and a boot-time drain; that is a separate seam from this ordering fix and larger than the per-PR cap. Verification: typecheck 0 errors; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings unchanged; new suite 4/4; multiplayer battery 73 suites / 532 tests; FULL unit suite 2415 suites / 33114 passed / 0 failed.
- [x] 7.2 Complete the bidirectional heartbeat contract in `Protocol.ts`, `ServerMatchSocketLifecycle.ts`, and `client.ts`; prove quiet healthy sessions remain connected and dead peers transition through reconnect.
  - Receipt (2026-08-24 — CLOSES this box): the contract was declared but only half-built, and the missing half was a LIVE DEFECT. `Protocol.ts` already documents Heartbeat as 'sent in BOTH directions' and already carries `HeartbeatSchema` in `ClientMessageSchema`, and the combat dispatcher already had a `case 'Heartbeat': return;` - so the server was ready to receive one. The CLIENT never sent one; `client.ts` handled the inbound ping with the comment 'clients do not need to echo'. But the server's liveness check is `Date.now() - lastInboundAt > HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `lastInboundAt` moves only on INBOUND traffic, so a player who was WATCHING rather than acting - a spectator, or anyone waiting out an opponent's turn - was reaped after 60s of their own silence and reconnected into the same silence. Written failing-test-first: the row asserting a quiet client emits heartbeats was RED before the change. FIX IS CLIENT-ONLY, which is the honest scope of this box: `startHeartbeat` fires at `HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS` (the server's own interval, so a single dropped frame still leaves two more inside the timeout), guards on `runtime.state.socket === socket && readyState === 1` so a reconnect's swapped-out socket is not written to forever, and swallows send failures because a failed keepalive is not worth surfacing when `onclose` reports the death properly. Cleared from BOTH close paths (`handleSocketClose` and `closeClient`) - a timer outliving its socket keeps the process alive and writes to a corpse. NEITHER `Protocol.ts` NOR `ServerMatchSocketLifecycle.ts` NEEDED A CHANGE, and that is stated rather than invented: both were read, and the server ping, the reaper, and the schema were already correct. 'QUIET HEALTHY SESSIONS REMAIN CONNECTED' is proven at both ends: the client row asserts a session idle for a full server timeout still emits more than one heartbeat, and a new lifecycle row drives the client's actual cadence against the reaper for FOUR consecutive timeout windows. That second row is what binds the two constants together - raise the client interval above the server timeout and it reds while every other row stays green. 'DEAD PEERS TRANSITION THROUGH RECONNECT' was already covered and is claimed only that far: the reaper closes the socket (pinned by 'detaches idle sockets after the heartbeat timeout window', which asserts `closeCount === 1`), and the client schedules a reconnect on close (pinned by 'schedules a reconnect on close (exponential backoff)'). Falsified 1:1 twice: neutralising the `startHeartbeat` call reds the client row and nothing else; removing the `noteInbound` from the cadence loop reds the cadence row, which is what shows it is not passing vacuously - the socket really would have died. A PAIRED ROW IS DELIBERATELY WEAK TODAY: 'stops heartbeating once the caller closes the client' passes trivially against a client that never heartbeats at all, and only became meaningful once the timer existed; it is kept because it pins the teardown, not the send. OUT OF SCOPE, NAMED: the CAMPAIGN socket has no liveness timer and its transport sends no heartbeats, so nothing there is reaped for silence - this box names the three combat files and is claimed no wider. - [x] 7.3 Add bounded per-connection queues to `ServerMatchBroadcaster.ts`; on saturation mark only that participant behind, continue healthy delivery, and resynchronize from the durable viewer cursor.
  - ERRATA (2026-09-02, finding #18, fixed by Wave A2b - box stays closed, its claim is now true on the fact path): the 2026-08-24 receipt's bound covered ServerMatchBroadcaster.broadcast ONLY. The per-viewer authorized event fan-out (ServerMatchHostEvents.publishEvent) resolved recipients from lifecycle.attachedSockets() and pushed each frame through broadcaster.safeSend, which applied no cap and consulted no behind set; during a live match broadcast carries typed errors, pause/resume and host migration - never command traffic - so a stalled viewer was handed authorized facts without limit and never entered the behind state at all. The bound now lives in ONE place, ServerMatchBroadcaster.admitForSend, and both fan-out paths ask it. Placement is load-bearing and was corrected against the orchestrator's first instruction: the per-viewer path asks AFTER deliveryCursors.assign, because ViewerDeliveryCursors distinguishes a frame never owed (consumes no number) from a frame owed and lost (consumes its number so the hole is a true loss signal) and firstMissedAuthoritySequence resumes the rejoin from exactly that number - the before-assign variant, run as a mutant, re-delivered already-applied frames (15 frames / 12 distinct). Proven on a real ServerMatchHost + real ViewerDeliveryCursors + the real replay path (ServerMatchHostEventsBackpressure.test.ts: saturated viewer refused while healthy viewers stay contiguous; refusal sticky until reaped; rejoin resumes at the first missed frame, no gap, no duplicate) - two of four rows RED on the pre-fix tree. Mutants killed: before-assign, behind socket not skipped, >= at the cap boundary. Contract preservation live: exactly-once-pack 4/4, restart-pack 2/2, resilience-pack 1/1 twice.

  - Receipt (2026-08-24 — CLOSES this box): THERE WAS NO BOUND AT ALL. `broadcast` called `socket.send` for every socket and swallowed failures; `ws` buffers what it cannot flush, without limit. One stalled client grew the server's memory for the length of a match, and every other recipient waited behind its `send` on the way past. THE QUEUE IS `bufferedAmount` - there is no queue of our own to bound, because `send` hands the payload straight to `ws`, so the bound is a check on the buffer `ws` already keeps. `MAX_BUFFERED_BYTES` is 1 MiB: far more than a healthy client ever holds (frames are small and drain in milliseconds) and far less than a stalled one reaches in a long match. ON SATURATION the connection is marked behind and skipped, and it STAYS skipped even after its buffer drains. That is deliberate and is the subtle half: resuming mid-stream would leave a HOLE in that connection's event sequence, and a hole it does not know about is worse than a gap it does. RESYNCHRONIZATION IS RECONNECT-AND-REPLAY, and the box is closed on that basis: the lifecycle's existing heartbeat tick now reaps a behind connection exactly as it reaps an idle one, the client's `onclose` schedules a reconnect (both already pinned), and its `SessionJoin` carries `lastSeq`, which `sendReplay` resumes from out of durable event storage. RESUMING FROM THE CLIENT'S CURSOR IS THE MORE CORRECT CHOICE, not a shortcut: the server's `deliveredCursor` records what was successfully SENT, while the client's `lastSeq` records what actually ARRIVED, and a frame can be sent into a dying socket and never parsed. The server-side cursor is preserved and exposed (`deliveredCursor`, `behindSockets`) because the spec asks for it and because 7.4's convergence gate is its first real consumer - stated plainly, NOTHING CONSUMES IT YET. A PROPERTY WORTH RECORDING: the bound applies to LIVE fan-out only. The join/replay path goes through `safeSend` (`ServerMatchHostReplay` lines 89-93), which has no saturation check, so a legitimate catch-up burst cannot trip the bound and put a client into a reconnect loop for the crime of needing a big replay. Verified by reading both paths in this session, not assumed. Side-tables are cleared on `unregister`, or they leak one row per connection for the life of the match. `IMatchSocket.bufferedAmount` is OPTIONAL: absent means 'no backpressure signal' and reads as healthy - every test double in the suite lacks it, and inventing saturation for a socket that cannot report it would disconnect all of them. Falsified 1:1 on each seam: neutralising the skip-if-behind reds 'keeps a behind connection behind even after its buffer drains'; neutralising the `unregister` cleanup reds 'forgets a connection entirely'; neutralising the lifecycle reap reds 'reaps a connection the broadcaster gave up on'; neutralising the saturation check reds FIVE rows, which is honest rather than tidy - it is the check the rest of the mechanism hangs off. Verification: typecheck 0 errors; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings unchanged; broadcaster + lifecycle suites 13/13; FULL unit suite 2414 suites / 33110 passed / 0 failed. Two of the three spec scenarios hold outright ('Slow player enters behind state', 'Healthy recipients continue'); the third ('Progression remains convergence-gated') is 7.4's and is NOT claimed here.

SLOW-CLIENT ISOLATION and BOUNDED QUEUE/MEMORY were delivered and falsified under 7.3 (#1359) - 'skips a saturated connection and keeps delivering to healthy ones', 'keeps a behind connection behind even after its buffer drains', 'forgets a connection entirely on unregister'. The spec scenario ('a player remains behind at a scenario or correction boundary -> delivery to healthy clients continues but launch of the next scenario remains blocked until convergence or audited GM removal') describes a gate that has not been built, and a test for it would either be vacuous or would be the feature itself smuggled in as a test. WHAT THE READING SHOWS. `launchCoopMission` composes the encounter and hands it to `launchCampaignEncounter`, which contains NO multiplayer references at all — it creates an encounter and a game session, never a match. And the destination itself carries no binding to recover one: `src/pages/gameplay/encounters/[id].tsx` and every page under `[id]/` (`pre-battle`, `select-force`, `sim`) contain no reference to `matchId`, `multiplayer`, or `coop`. Both were right, and building the seated match turned up a live defect that is broader than the co-op question that prompted it. MEASURED, NOT INFERRED. On a properly-seated 1v1 — `meta.seats` occupied, server-derived ownership resolving to `ownedForceIds=["alpha-1"]` for one player and `["bravo-1"]` for the other — the player holding the BRAVO (opponent) seat issued a Move for `unit-A2`, a PLAYER-side mech, and the host committed it: `movement_declared` + `movement_locked`, position `(-1,5)` -> `(-1,4)`. The control in the same run (the owner moving their own unit) also committed, which is what makes the result readable: the harness is faithful, so the refusal is the thing that was missing. NEITHER EXISTING LAYER COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT, and this is structural rather than a missed branch. A Move names `unitId`, never `forceId`, so `commandRequestFromIntent` builds an EMPTY force claim and the subset check against `ownedForceIds` passes trivially — the comment right above it ("unit ids are not force ids") records the decision that left it that way. The engine cannot catch it either: `EngineIntentHandler` is `(session, intent) => void`, so the dispatch layer never learns who sent the command. Ownership had nowhere to be checked. WHY IT SURVIVED THIS LONG, which is the part worth keeping: `ownsCoopUnit` opened by asserting that "`ServerMatchHost` already validates unit ownership for any seated match". That was false when it was written. It made the co-op helper look like a refinement of a working base case instead of the only ownership check in the codebase, and the base case was never built. The comment is corrected in the same commit, because leaving it would re-seed the same mistake in the next reader. THE FIX is a side-granularity refusal at the intent seam, before rate-limiting and before any engine dispatch: resolve the actor unit's side from engine state, resolve the caller's side from `meta.sideAssignments`, refuse `AUTH_REJECTED`/`unit-not-owned` when they differ. Both halves are records the server already owns, so nothing new is threaded through the wire. The actor is `unitId`, or `attackerId` for Attack/Physical; `targetId` is pointedly NOT read, since shooting a unit you do not own is the entire game. Server-internal callers are exempt — the host acting is not a principal claiming scope. Falsified 1:1 (#PR): neutralizing only the side comparison reds exactly the refusal row and leaves the owner-control row green, and the row also asserts the unit DID NOT MOVE, so a guard that refused loudly while committing anyway would not pass. WHAT THIS DOES **NOT** CLOSE, stated plainly so the box is not read as done. (1) It separates OPPONENTS, not TEAMMATES. In a co-op match both players are assigned side `player`, so caller side and unit side match and the guard returns without refusing — player B commanding player A's mech is still accepted. That intra-side grain is exactly what `ownsCoopUnit` expresses, and it is STILL uncalled, because `coopSeats` still never leave `composeCoopEncounter` and so the per-unit owner map still never reaches the host. Nothing in this step changed that. (2) A match whose meta carries no `sideAssignments` resolves no caller side and is left exactly as it was rather than newly refused — deliberate, since making unresolved-ownership matches unplayable is a bet this slice did not verify across every creation path. (3) The proposal / readiness / launch clauses of this task are unaffected by this step. CORRECTION TO THE PREVIOUS NOTE, which got the reason right but the evidence wrong: it reported that the earlier probe saw own/teammate/enemy all "commit". They did not. Re-run with the effect measured instead of the frame kind, all three were `movement_invalid` events — engine refusals for `NoMovementCapability`, because the probe's units were `as IGameUnit` casts with no movement stats — and no position changed. `kind: 'Event'` was never evidence of success; an engine REFUSAL is also an Event. The empty `ownedForceIds` was real, but it was not what made that probe unreadable.
  - Progress (2026-08-26, third step — DECISION AUTHORITY closed; a LAUNCH-side gap is recorded but NOT claimed as an exploit): the decision clause is done and was a real hole - `handleCampaignDecision` never checked the sender, so in `host-review` mode (the default the co-op entry panel picks) a guest could submit a proposal and immediately approve their own, committing it with the GM never consulted. `handleCampaignHostIntent` had always compared against `entry.hostPlayerId`; the decision path fifty lines away simply never did. Fixed with the same comparison and `AUTH_REJECTED`/`campaign-decision-requires-gm`; the row also asserts the proposal is STILL PENDING after the refusal, so a guard that complained while deciding anyway would not pass. Falsified 1:1 (#1382). ON THE LAUNCH CLAUSE, stated carefully because I could not finish verifying it: three facts ARE established by reading. (1) `ownsCoopUnit` - the per-unit co-op ownership predicate - has NO production caller; only its own tests reference it. (2) `coopSeats`, which `composeCoopEncounter` tags with `ownerPlayerId`, never leave that module: `launchCoopMission` forwards only `composition.encounter` onward, so no per-unit ownership data reaches `ServerMatchHost` at all. (3) Unit-scoped intents carry no force identity - `MoveIntentSchema` is `unitId`/`to`/`facing`/`movementType` - while `commandRequestFromIntent` builds its authorization request from `claimedForceIds` and `claimedParticipantId`, so FORCE-SCOPE authorization has nothing to constrain a unit-scoped command with. WHAT IS **NOT** ESTABLISHED, and must not be read into the above: that a co-op player can actually command a teammate's or an opponent's unit in production. I probed it and the probe was NOT REPRESENTATIVE - the harness created a match with `sideAssignments` but no seats or server-derived force ownership, so `ownedForceIds` was empty and an empty claim passed trivially; all three of own/teammate/enemy committed, INCLUDING the enemy case, which is itself the signal that the harness rather than the product was permissive. `HumanActionAuthorization` has real coverage that contradicts a naive reading of that result ('command force scope must be a subset of server-derived ownedForceIds', 'an admitted member whose membership was revoked cannot command', 'a mismatched participant claim is scope-escalation, never a fallback'). ANYONE PICKING THIS UP SHOULD BUILD A PROPERLY-SEATED CO-OP MATCH FIRST - real seats, real server-derived force ownership, both players on the same side - and only then decide whether unit-scoped commands are constrained. The probe was deleted rather than merged, because a test that cannot tell its own subject apart from its harness is worse than none.

- [x] 9.3 Implement durable GM-loss pause with no implicit player promotion; allow resume only by the same reauthenticated GM and participant removal only through an audited GM command.
  - DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED (2026-09-03, audit completeness pass at a98e70e01): this box was CLOSED while the match tier did the exact thing its letter forbids. onLastSocketDropped calls handleSocketDropped, which awaited migrateHostIfNeeded FIRST; when the dropped player held meta.hostPlayerId, HostMigration picked the longest-connected human seat occupant, PERSISTED updateMatchMeta({ hostPlayerId: successor }) and broadcast HostMigrated - and its docstring said the migration stays migrated even if the original host reconnects. Because commandAuthorityProjection derives viewerRole and authority 'host-gm' and the NETWORK_HOST_CONTROLS set (approve, gm-correction) purely from hostPlayerId === playerId, and NetworkedGameSurface gates the GM control block on that role, the drop really handed GM powers to a tactical player and never gave them back. The GM is the host of a co-op campaign match (matches/index.ts passes gmParticipantId: hostPlayerId and sets the creating GM as host), so this was reachable on every co-op session. The original receipt reasoned only about bindCampaignSyncConnection's host branch; the match channel is a second door and was ungated. THE FIX: an optional ICampaignGmHostProbe on the migration context, bound at ServerMatchHost construction, reads the DURABLE seat - activeCampaignSessionMembership for (campaignId, sessionId = matchId, participantId), whose row the creation checkpoint binds as seat 'gm' under a migration that constrains seat to gm or player - and migration refuses with { migrated: false, reason: 'campaign-gm' } when the dropped host holds it. The pause path still runs, and because hostPlayerId never changes the same GM reconnecting is the host again, which is the letter's 'same reauthenticated GM' clause. Absent signal is byte-identical to before: no coop campaign on the meta, no probe bound, an unready participant store, or no gm row all fall through to today's migration, which ordinary tactical matches still need. The durable seat was chosen over the easy 'skip every co-op match' test because the seat is the GM identity; the host id is only where it happens to sit. Rows: a dropped GM leaves hostPlayerId unchanged, broadcasts no HostMigrated and answers campaign-gm; the same GM reconnecting still projects host-gm with approve and gm-correction; a GM drop pauses the match; a non-GM host still migrates (control); a co-op match with no gm row still migrates (absent-signal pin). Orchestrator mutants in the PR body, including one that restores the original defect. NOT CLAIMED: the projection and the surface are unchanged (correct given a correct host id); no GM field was added to IMatchMeta; the campaign-tier session pause is out of scope; and 9.1's separate finding - that the GM occupies one of the two tactical human seats - is filed as errata, not fixed here.

It is also TERMINAL by design and by its own test ('pauses the session and closes the host' asserts `host.isClosed()`), so wiring it up would have made GM loss unrecoverable rather than paused. A recoverable pause now exists beside it: `noteGmConnected` / `noteGmDisconnected`, wired from the campaign socket's HOST branch and its cleanup. Only the GM's own connection can reach either, because `role` is host precisely when the connection's VERIFIED principal matches the registered host - a tactical player cannot claim it, which is what makes 'no implicit promotion' structural rather than a rule someone has to remember. PRESENCE IS A COUNT, NOT A FLAG, and the falsification is what forced that: with a flag, the `!gmPresent` guard was unreachable and neutralising it reddened nothing - which turned out to be hiding a real defect. A GM holding TWO connections (a second tab, or a reconnect landing before the old socket's close is processed) paused a session they were still sitting in the moment either one closed. Now paused only when the LAST GM connection goes. THE PAUSE BITES: `CampaignProposal`, `CampaignDecision`, and `CampaignHostIntent` are refused while paused with `MATCH_PAUSED` / `campaign-paused-gm-absent` - a code that already means exactly this, so none was minted. Join, grant-join, and acknowledgement stay OPEN on purpose: those are how a participant gets back and how the GM returns to resume, and refusing them would make the pause unrecoverable. Five rows pin the behaviour: the GM dropping pauses; a player's command is refused while the GM is absent; a player RECONNECTING does not resume it; the same GM reconnecting does; and a non-GM connection closing does not pause at all. Falsified 1:1: dropping the pause assignment reds four rows, dropping the resume reds one, and forcing the refusal helper to admit reds one. WHY THIS BOX STAYS OPEN, EXACTLY: (1) DURABILITY. The paused state lives on the in-memory session, so a process restart forgets it - a restarted server rehydrates the campaign unpaused with no GM connected. Making it durable means a column on the session row and a read on rehydration, which is 9.1's schema work. (2) AUDITED PARTICIPANT REMOVAL. Not built at all: revocation exists in the participant store (6.1) but there is no GM command that performs it and no audit record of who removed whom. (3) 'MAY resume after cursor convergence' from the spec scenario is NOT implemented - the resume here is immediate on the GM's return, and gating it on convergence is 9.4's gate, which does not exist. - [x] 15.1 Implement immutable checkpoints keyed by branch, authority head, reducer version, and digest, satisfying `Checkpoints and Compaction Are Cache-Only`.
  - Receipt (2026-09-02, Wave D1 seam 1 - CLOSES this box): the checkpoint CONTRACT (ReplayCheckpointCompatibility, ReplayEquivalenceHarness.recoverState with the digestsVerified law, SQLiteReplayCheckpointRepository, migration 10) had NO producer - the harness header and #1518's candidate verification both say so. src/lib/events/checkpoints/BranchCheckpointCache.ts is the producer: a checkpoint's id is DERIVED from the key it binds (streamType+streamId encoded the way ReplayQuarantineRegistry scopes keys, branch, authority head revision, projectorId + projectorVersion, schema pipeline fingerprint), so the same key is the same primary key and a second write is a collision the writer answers for - the identical claim is 'already-recorded' (proven through selectRecoveryBase's digest law, not by trusting the row), a different claim is the repository's typed duplicate-checkpoint refusal, nothing is ever overwritten; a bumped projector version writes a NEW row beside the old (the superseded one stays for the manifest). The reader hands out an ICandidateCheckpointOffer - no state field exists on the return type; expected.sourceTailDigest is the LIVE chain digest at that revision (never the row's own claim - that would forge digestsVerified), expected.stateDigest comes from the row only because hydrate already proved the stored bytes hash to it. Only recoverState turns an offer into state, and because the accelerated path reads only the TAIL, the kernel's full-replay fallback would fold a partial history - a base that verified and then failed is a typed accelerated-recovery-rejected, never a state (the load-bearing rule of the seam). CACHE-ONLY proven: with the table emptied, recovery takes the reference path and returns the same state and the same canonical digest, differing only in appliedRevisions. Earlier trusted base: the reader walks candidates newest-first and passes over a row live history cannot attest. Red-first: module-not-found. Worker mutants killed (5): row overwritten in place, key without the projector version, state handed out bypassing recoverState, deleting checkpoints changing an observable, reader ignoring the branch; one documented survivor (discovery dropping the projector version - selectRecoveryBase re-filters; a cost, not a hole; stated in code). NOT claimed: a checkpoint on a non-root branch (migration 10 pins branch_id='root' mirroring event_journal_events; lifting one without the other would cache branches whose events cannot exist - both belong to the branches leaf's activation, its 3c cut in flight; the key/writer/reader are branch-generic and the branch keying is proven behaviourally); no production call site records or recovers yet (session-recovery wiring is 15.2/15.3); viewer digests (15.2) and quarantine (15.4) are later seams. ## 17. Coordinated Post-Receipt Outcome Correction

- [ ] 17.1 Implement a distinct higher-version coordinated outcome-correction command for the post-campaign-receipt boundary, satisfying `Coordinated Outcome Correction Supersedes Across Journals`.
  - PROGRESS (2026-09-02, Wave C6 17.1-a): the DISTINCT command exists as a typed module (CoordinatedOutcomeCorrection.ts): kind CoordinatedOutcomeCorrection carrying the delivered outcomeId, the NEW outcomeVersion as a version DISCRIMINATOR (it must be exactly delivered+1, never a free number), targetRevision, the same four expected* head fields the rewind commit carries, and the actor. Admission order is the contract: GM authority (post-combat fix) -> the inbox's MAX(outcome_version) for the outcome, refused outcome-not-delivered when nothing was delivered (the mirror of 13.4: this door exists only after delivery; before it the plain rewind is the door) -> version-not-next unless delivered+1 -> no-authoritative-history -> the shipped expected-head validation (STALE_BRANCH / STALE_REVISION / STALE_GENERATION) -> accepted-pending-saga. Admission WRITES NOTHING; the typed result is the receipt 17.2 will consume. A nine-member refusal Record is phrased (LAW 40). Rows (host harness on the rewind-commit SQLite style): the GM correction at delivered+1 is accepted-pending-saga and writes only the admission (census); version delivered+2 is refused version-not-next; an undelivered outcome is refused outcome-not-delivered; a non-GM is refused gm-role-required; a stale expected revision is refused STALE_REVISION; the plain rewind onto a delivered receipt is STILL refused campaign-receipt-delivered (pin). Orchestrator mutants in the PR body. NOT CLAIMED, and why 17.1 stays open: the command is not yet on the wire (no Protocol intent kind, no host/route door - the intent pipeline dispatches every admitted combat intent to the engine and this command must not be dispatched), so no live actor can send it; that door lands with the saga (17.2) so an admitted command is never left with nothing to consume it. No replacement outbox, no N+1 gate (17.3), no GameEvent.

- [ ] 17.2 Execute coordinated correction as a durable saga: atomically record combat supersession, invalidation manifest, and replacement outcome outbox in the source authority, then idempotently record the target-scoped campaign replacement receipt and deterministic consequence batch in the target authority; expose pending/blocked state and do not claim a cross-store transaction.
  - PROGRESS (2026-09-02, Wave E17 17.2-a): the SOURCE half of the saga exists as a typed module (CoordinatedOutcomeCorrectionSaga.ts) with its durable state. A saga table on the MATCH STORE database, mp_coordinated_correction_saga (PRIMARY KEY (match_id, outcome_id, outcome_version) = the admission's version discriminator; state source-recorded | manifest-sealed | target-pending | completed | blocked with blocked_reason and per-step timestamps; idempotent CREATE migration in the supersession style). Step 1, recordCoordinatedCorrectionSource, is ONE match-store transaction: the saga row in source-recorded, the tail moved by 14.4-b's supersedeMatchStoreFrom from the first superseded sequence (revision = sequence + 1, the rebuild's rule), and the match's ONE outcome slot in mp_combat_outcome_outbox (match_id is its PRIMARY KEY - finding #103: the worker's first cut inserted a second row and would have collided on any real match; its suite had seeded no delivered outcome) REPLACED by the accepted higher version as a fresh pending publication (published_at NULL); an injected failure on the slot write leaves the live table intact and no saga row. A retry with the same key is a no-op that answers the recorded row (one supersession, one slot write); a slot already at the accepted version with the same payload is adopted; the same version with a different payload, or a newer correction already holding the slot, is refused typed replacement-immutable. Step 2, sealCoordinatedCorrectionManifest, is on the JOURNAL database (sealArtifactManifest on the correction's candidate branch, skipped when already sealed) and then advances the saga row to manifest-sealed only from source-recorded. The two steps are separately durable and no cross-store transaction is claimed: a crash between them is a saga row that still reads source-recorded, which is the exposed pending state; blockCoordinatedCorrection answers blocked with its reason. Rows: source record moves the tail, writes the slot and the saga row together; injected slot failure leaves no partial state; a delivered outcome (version N, published) is replaced by N+1 pending; a slot already at N+1 with the same payload is adopted; a slot at N+2 refuses; sealing a blocked saga does not advance it; same-key retry is a no-op; different payload at the same version refused typed; the replacement row is pending and is NOT a match publication (listPendingPublications reads mp_match_outbox only - combat replacements ride getCombatOutcomeOutbox / markCombatOutcomePublished); read after the source step only answers source-recorded; the seal advances the saga and a second seal is equal; block answers blocked with the reason. NOT CLAIMED, why 17.2 stays open: the TARGET side (17.2-b: the target-scoped campaign replacement receipt and the deterministic consequence batch in the campaign authority, idempotent, advancing target-pending -> completed) is not built - those two states are union members only; nothing routes the admission's accepted-pending-saga into this saga yet (no route, no intent kind); the scenario N+1 gate is 17.3; retry/reconnect/restart proofs are 17.4.
  - PROGRESS (2026-09-03, Wave E17 17.2-b): the TARGET half now exists, so both authorities of the saga are written. recordCoordinatedCorrectionTarget runs only on a saga the source half left at manifest-sealed (any other state, or none, answers typed not-ready and writes nothing), and its first act is to look for an existing replacement receipt keyed (outcome_id, outcome_version): a hit returns that receipt with no writes, which is the retry law made observable. The ordered steps and their keys: acquire the campaign correction lease on campaignStreamRef(campaignId); mint the correction candidate; PERSIST candidate_branch_id ON THE SAGA ROW BEFORE ANY JOURNAL WRITE (the branch id is random and not derivable, so a crash after the mint would otherwise leave an orphan branch and a retry would mint a second - the saga column is added by a table_info-guarded ALTER so a match store that already ran 17.2-a keeps its rows); replay the retained source commands onto the candidate; append the deterministic consequence batch under the candidate-scoped command id ${candidateBranchId}--outcome-correction:${outcomeId}:${outcomeVersion}; INSERT OR IGNORE the replacement receipt into the new append-only campaign_combat_outcome_replacement table (keyed (outcome_id, outcome_version), carrying the campaign, the candidate branch, the command and its revision range) - the ACCEPTED INBOX ROW IS NEVER UPDATED, because 21.3's immutable-correction letter and D6/D7 say a correction supersedes by a new fact and prior history stays auditable; seal the campaign impact if it is not already sealed; advance the saga to target-pending. The campaign inbox is deliberately NOT the door for the replacement: it is keyed by outcome_id with one accepted version and answers outcome-version-conflict for any other, and it is not branch-aware (its batch defaults to root), so pushing N+1 through it would either be refused or land on the wrong branch. Retryability is separated from failure: a lease held by someone else answers not-ready with the reason (a retry can succeed), while a non-retryable refusal blocks the saga with its reason. A replayed group whose candidate-scoped command id already landed is treated as convergence rather than an error, so a re-run of the whole target step is a no-op. The GM 'pending' posture stays jest-only by decision: proposals are in-memory and arrive from a guest over the transport, so a postLoadAction could only reach it via page.evaluate, which breaks the front-door promise the loader header makes (design D8); it becomes sweepable only when the sweep is two-browser (21.x/25.x).

  - Progress (2026-09-02, Wave E2 seam 1 - box stays OPEN for the a11y-per-posture half of seam 2): `npm run verify:qc:viewport-sweep` run by the orchestrator in the e2e worktree at the PR A head 74bd093a6 - 6 + 12 + 51 passed (4.0m), incl. every pack-seeded campaign route and the mission launch briefing; the affected component suites (src/components/campaign 27 suites / 200, plus common/multiplayer/lifecycle trees 148 suites / 1152) green at both heads. Accessibility assertions exist for every posture shipped so far (polite live regions incl. blocked, on all three surfaces); the keyboard-complete confirmation shell, focus management and narrow-viewport primary actions (19.3) and their assertions are seam 2.

  - Progress (2026-08-24, STAYS OPEN — see the blocker at the end): found and fixed a real accessibility defect while writing the assertions this task asks for. `CampaignSyncStateBanner` — the strip that tells a player whether to trust what is on screen — was a plain `<p>` with NO live region. A sighted player watches it flip from "up to date" to "reconnecting"; a screen-reader user got nothing at all, because a status that changes outside a live region is announced to nobody. It now carries `role="status"`, `aria-live="polite"`, and `aria-atomic="true"`. POLITE DELIBERATELY, including for `blocked`: assertive cuts off whatever the reader is currently speaking, a sync posture is never more urgent than the sentence the player is in the middle of, and the blocked state also disables the controls so it is discoverable by trying rather than only by hearing. ATOMIC because without it a reader may announce only the CHANGED words, turning "you are up to date" into a fragment that means nothing alone. New a11y suite (`CampaignSyncStateBanner.a11y.test.tsx`, 14 rows, jest `a11y` project): axe-clean for all SIX existing states rather than one representative, because the per-state tone classes are exactly the kind of thing that silently breaks contrast for a single posture - and the broken one would be a failure state, which is when the message matters most; live-region announcement asserted BY ROLE rather than by testid, so it pins the accessibility tree a screen reader actually walks instead of a hook only tests can see; an explicit row that the region is never assertive; and a row that no state's announcement contains a DIGIT, because a spoken "12 events behind" rebuilds the same inference channel tasks 3.2/3.4 closed for the printed one. Falsified 1:1: changing `role="status"` to `role="presentation"` turns 8 of the 14 rows red. Verification: tsc clean; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings unchanged; a11y project 7 suites / 53 tests; campaign-coop + replica units 15 suites / 77 tests; **`npm run verify:qc:viewport-sweep` 51 passed (3.5m)**, which is the second of this task's three named gates; FULL unit suite 2410 suites / 33061 passed / 0 failed. **WHY THIS BOX STAYS OPEN:** the task requires a11y assertions for EVERY lifecycle state, and 19.1 names nine — pending, sealed, finalized, syncing, reconnecting, behind, rebuilding, rewound, blocked. Four of those cannot be asserted because they do not exist to render: a case-insensitive grep over non-test `src/components` and `src/lib/campaign` finds `sealed` in 0 files and `rewound` in 0 files, with `finalized` (1) and `rebuilding` (2) present only outside the lifecycle UI. Writing assertions for states nothing renders would be theatre. The six states that DO exist are now covered end to end; 19.4 closes when 19.1 builds the remaining four, and this note is the record of exactly which ones are owed.


## 20. Three-Context Sandbox Foundation

- [x] 20.1 Add `e2e/fixtures/gmTwoPlayerCampaign.ts` with isolated contexts reserved for the future non-playing GM, Player 1, and Player 2, plus a harness-owned server, per-run database, distinct identities, and deterministic seeds. The foundation proves isolation first; Task 9 durable membership later binds those contexts to actual roles and must pass `membership-smoke` before role admission is claimed.
- [x] 20.2 Add test-only fault controls guarded by `NODE_ENV === 'test'`, E2E run ID, explicit session scope, and one-shot consumption; make production startup reject enabled controls.
  - Receipt (2026-08-24): `src/lib/testing/faultControls.ts`. A fault-injection switch is a remote kill switch wearing a helpful name, so arming one requires FOUR things to line up and each closes a different leak: the build must be `NODE_ENV === 'test'`; a RUN id, so one Playwright run cannot arm a seam another run then trips over (which is what makes parallel runs safe); a SESSION scope, so the fault hits one session and a healthy control session stays available to prove the failure was CONTAINED rather than global; and ONE-SHOT consumption, because an armed-until-disarmed switch outlives the test that armed it and fails the next one for no visible reason. Arming outside a test build THROWS rather than no-opping - a silent no-op would let a fault test "pass" on a production build having proven nothing at all - and consumption returns false for every near miss (wrong run, wrong session, wrong seam, already consumed, not a test build), because a seam that fires on an approximate match is worse than no seam. A partially-scoped control is refused outright: it looks armed and its blast radius is whatever the missing field would have narrowed. PRODUCTION STARTUP REFUSES, and refuses at the REAL entrypoint: `server.js` now exits 1 with `FAULT_CONTROLS_IN_PRODUCTION` when `MEKSTATION_FAULT_CONTROLS` is set outside a test build. Exiting rather than ignoring is deliberate - a process that boots and merely declines to honour fault config is one refactor away from honouring it, while one that refuses to start cannot rot that way. The guard is a hand-written MIRROR because `server.js` is CommonJS and cannot import the TS module, so a sibling test SPAWNS `server.js` itself rather than trusting the mirror; drift between the two would otherwise be invisible. The guard is "not a test build", not "is production", so a dev server carrying fault config is refused identically. Environment is INJECTED rather than read globally, so exercising the production path cannot disturb other tests sharing the worker (it also removed a `NODE_ENV` readonly type error rather than casting past it). Falsified 1:1: neutralising the startup guard turns both rejection rows red while the no-config CONTROL row stays green - without that control every rejection row would pass against a server that simply fails to boot for an unrelated reason, which on a machine with no build it otherwise would. Verification: tsc clean; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings unchanged; fault-control suites 19/19 (16 scoping + 3 real-process startup); FULL unit suite 2408 suites / 33037 passed / 0 failed. NON-CLAIM: this ships the CONTROL, not the seams. No production call site consumes a fault yet - wiring `store.commit`, `outbox.insert`, and head-update seams belongs to the tasks that build those paths (plan todo 28 and leaf-4 task 2.4), and `MEKSTATION_FAULT_CONTROLS` is currently read only by the startup refusal.

- [x] 20.3 Add a dedicated SQLite evidence reader opened `readonly: true, fileMustExist: true`; never instantiate the production store for read-only proof.
  - Receipt (2026-08-24): `e2e/fixtures/sqliteEvidenceReader.ts` opens its own connection with `readonly: true, fileMustExist: true` and imports NOTHING from the production persistence layer. Both options are load-bearing for a reason worth stating: the production store CREATES its file and runs migrations, so pointed at a database the run never produced it would manufacture an empty one and report zero rows - evidence of absence conjured from nothing, which is the exact shape of a false pass. `fileMustExist` turns that into a thrown `EVIDENCE_DB_MISSING`, and the row proving it asserts the file was NOT brought into existence, not merely that the open threw. `readonly` makes immutability a database-level guarantee rather than a convention, and the byte-equality row compares the file BEFORE and AFTER a full probe (count + select + tables) as bytes rather than row counts, because a probe that rewrote a page while leaving rows alone would still make the artifact describe a different file than the run produced - which is what the plan's evidence gate means by identical hash and schema. Two refusals are named rather than left to the driver: a non-SELECT statement is rejected as `EVIDENCE_WRITE_REFUSED` with the offending SQL (the connection would refuse it anyway, but as "attempt to write a readonly database", which does not say what was attempted), and a table name that could carry SQL is rejected outright because table names cannot be bound as parameters. WIRED, NOT SHELVED: the fixture exposes `openEvidence('app' | 'multiplayer')` over the run-owned paths `assertRunOwnedPath` already validated, and the `fixture-smoke` gate now reads its own database through it and asserts the hash is unchanged - so the reader is proven against REAL run databases end to end, not only against synthetic ones. Falsified 1:1: flipping `fileMustExist`/`readonly` off turns the missing-database row red. A SELF-CORRECTION worth recording: the guard proving no production dependency first banned the string `DurableMatchStore` anywhere in the file, and failed on the module's own comment EXPLAINING why it is not used; it now scans import lines only, so it fails on a dependency rather than on prose. Verification: tsc clean; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings unchanged; reader suite 6/6; `fixture-smoke` and the 20.5 ownership spec both green on real builds; FULL unit suite 2405 suites / 33011 passed / 0 failed. NON-CLAIM: this ships the reader and one live use of it; the per-table durable-row assertions the program's evidence bundle needs (receipts, batches, outbox, participants, cursors, branches, outcome receipts) arrive with the tasks that create those tables.

- [x] 20.4 Add role-labeled traces, screenshots, raw socket transcript, pre-serialization projection capture, latency timestamps, durable-row export, state/projection hashes, environment manifest, and cleanup log under `test-results/gm-two-player/<run-id>/`.
  - Receipt (2026-08-24): `e2e/fixtures/gmTwoPlayerEvidence.ts` writes the declared bundle under `test-results/gm-two-player/<run-id>/`, role-labeled in the FILENAME so three contexts are distinguishable from a directory listing rather than by opening every file, with a sha256 and byte count per artifact. Two properties make such a bundle trustworthy and both are easy to lose silently. (1) IT CANNOT WRITE OUTSIDE ITS RUN - every path goes through the same `assertRunOwnedPath` the sandbox already uses, because a writer that escapes clobbers another run's evidence or a developer's files and the resulting artifact describes a run that never happened; a `../../../` name is refused. (2) IT CANNOT CARRY SECRETS - the bundle captures raw socket transcripts and pre-serialization projections, which is exactly where a bearer token or password lives, and an evidence file is written to disk, uploaded as a CI artifact, and read by people who were not in the run, so a leak there is a leak everywhere. The scan matches the SHAPE of a credential (password/token fields, `Bearer`, private keys) rather than known values, because a list of known secrets contains only the ones already known about; the refusal does NOT echo the match into its error, since an error message is itself logged and repeating it would defeat the refusal it just performed. A CONTROL row writes ordinary prose that merely mentions "token" and must still be accepted - a guard that refused every transcript containing the word would be switched off within a week. (3) THE MANIFEST DECLARES INTENT, not inventory: `recordMissing` puts an artifact the run could not capture into the manifest with a reason, so a missing file is a DETECTABLE gap; a manifest listing only what exists cannot distinguish "never captured" from "captured and lost", which is the difference between a gap and a bug. WIRED AND PROVEN LIVE: the fixture hands out `openEvidenceBundle()` and the `fixture-smoke` gate now writes a per-role artifact and finalizes a manifest; verified by reading the real bundle off disk after the run (3 role-labeled files plus `manifest.json`, every sha256 64 hex chars, environment carrying node version and run id). Falsified 1:1: neutralising the secret scan and the run-owned path check turns 6 rows red while the 4 structural rows stay green. Verification: tsc clean; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings unchanged; bundle suite 10/10; `fixture-smoke` green end to end on a real build; FULL unit suite 2409 suites / 33047 passed / 0 failed. SECTION 20 IS NOW COMPLETE (20.1-20.5). NON-CLAIMS: the bundle is the WRITER plus one live use - actually capturing traces, screenshots, socket transcripts, latency series, and durable-row exports happens in the tasks that produce those things (Playwright tracing/screenshots ride Todo 26's runner; socket transcripts and pre-serialization projections need the seams of sections 5 and 11; durable-row export needs the tables of section 2). The secret scan is a shape heuristic, not a proof of absence: it catches the credentials this program actually handles and would not catch an unrecognised encoding.

- [x] 20.5 Add ownership-scoped cleanup that preserves ambient browser tabs, Chrome processes, unrelated servers, databases, and user artifacts.
  - Receipt (2026-08-24): writing the proof found a REAL defect rather than confirming the implementation. The context half was already scoped correctly - `cleanup()` closes only the three contexts it opened - but the IDENTITY half was not, and nothing had ever asserted it. Seeding an identity through `POST /api/e2e/vault-identity` calls `repository.setActive`, whose SQL is `UPDATE vault_identities SET is_active = 0` across the WHOLE table; the sandbox seeds three, so it deactivated whatever identity the machine had, and then deleted its own three on teardown. End state: NO active identity at all. `getActive()` returned null and `/api/multiplayer/auth/token` answered `404 No vault identity configured` - a developer's own vault session silently stopped working after running this suite, which is exactly the "preserve ... user artifacts" this task forbids. FIX: the fixture now reads the prior active id BEFORE its first seed (new read-only `GET /api/e2e/vault-identity`, id-only because the caller needs to restore the identity, not learn about it) and hands it back through `DELETE`'s new `restoreActiveId`, which reactivates AFTER the deletes so the final state is the one the caller asked for even when the restore target was also in the delete list. A restore target that no longer exists is REPORTED (`restored: false`), not thrown - the harness cannot know whether something else removed that identity meanwhile, and failing teardown over it would turn a tidy-up into a test failure. PROOF (`e2e/gm-two-player-cleanup-ownership.spec.ts`, 2/2 on a real build): an ambient context and an ambient active identity survive a full fixture lifecycle; the fixture's own three contexts are gone and the bystander's is not; the ambient PAGE is re-navigated rather than merely found in the list, because a context object can outlive its pages; and a second `cleanup()` destroys nothing further (specs that clean up in both `finally` and an error path call it twice). A control asserts the fixture actually ADDED contexts, so the preservation rows cannot pass against a fixture that created nothing. Falsified 1:1 - dropping `restoreActiveId` turns both rows red. HONEST NOTE ON METHOD: the first version of this spec probed identity liveness by minting a token and asserted an "unrelated identity survives", which failed - and the failure was mine, not the fixture's: identities are a collection with ONE active row, so `getActive()`-backed auth answers about activation, not existence. Chasing that wrong probe is what surfaced the actual defect. Verification: tsc clean; oxlint 0 errors / 78 warnings, unchanged from main at `c7fba3db7` (the count moved 77 to 78 when PRs #1342-#1343 landed, not here); api/vault/scripts batteries 117 suites / 1784 tests; the endpoint's own unit suite grew 4 to 8 rows covering the GET, the null case, a successful restore, and an unrestorable target; the `fixture-smoke` gate still passes end to end; FULL unit suite 2404 suites / 33005 passed / 0 failed. NON-CLAIMS: Chrome PROCESS preservation and unrelated-server/database preservation are asserted only insofar as the fixture never targets them - there is no positive process-level assertion, and adding one needs the fault-control seams of task 20.2; this closes the browser-tab and user-artifact halves of 20.5 with evidence and leaves the process half named rather than implied.

The bound is UNACKED FRAMES, not bytes and not socket.bufferedAmount (#19: that stays 0 on match traffic): unacked = issued - 1 - lastAcked (issued when never acked); MAX_VIEWER_UNACKED = 64 on the cursors module (a healthy client acks within a handful of frames; the cap absorbs a hitch or one phase-advance burst; Infinity would never isolate). ViewerDeliveryCursors.admit runs once, before assign, in the assign/send loop: when a viewer's unacked window reaches the cap THAT viewer is isolated (no further live assign/send; the first refused authority is remembered so firstMissedAuthoritySequence names the gap) while every other viewer keeps being assigned; handleDeliveryAck clears the isolation when unacked drops under the cap and resumes the viewer through the SAME path a reconnect uses (handleSessionJoin from firstMissedAuthoritySequence) - never from the live head. Rows: unacked counts issued minus acked and trips the bound for that viewer only; a fresh ack below the bound clears the isolation; withholding Player 2's acks isolates Player 2 while Player 1's issued keeps growing (the harness had to learn that handleIntent rate-limits per connection - 20 intents per bucket - so the drive uses a fresh connection id per step and a turnLimit of 0; a guard now names the last frame kind if a drive ever stops issuing); one ack from the isolated viewer resumes it from firstMissedAuthoritySequence, not the live head; the GM is never isolated by another viewer's backlog.   - FINDING #96 (2026-09-02, Wave C3 3c-heads, CONFIRMED by an it.failing row, not inferred): boot recovery treats a C1a-seeded candidate head as journal corruption. SQLiteEventJournalRecovery.verifyHeads compares the head-row count to the number of distinct (stream, branch) event chains derived from stored events; a candidate head seeded at its base revision has a row but no events on that branch yet, so heads = 2 and chains = 1 and recovery answers corrupt('Stream head count differs'). Row: SQLiteEventJournalRecovery.test.ts 'finding #86-adjacent: a C1a candidate head with no events is not journal corruption' (it.failing until fixed). Latent today - nothing in production mints a candidate yet - but it MUST be fixed before 3b-iv ships a rewind commit, since the first rewind would make the next boot refuse the journal. Owner: its own seam (recovery must reconcile candidate head rows against event_history_branches - a branch whose head sits at its recorded base with zero events is a seed, not corruption); #86 CLOSED by this seam.
  - CLOSURES (2026-09-02, Wave C4 recovery reconcile): #96 FIXED and #97 FIXED, one seam. #96: SQLiteEventJournalRecovery partitions head rows into event-backed rows (compared to the event-derived (stream, branch) chains exactly as before) and SEED rows; a seed is legitimate iff its branch exists in event_history_branches AND the row's stream_revision equals that branch's recorded base revision AND its event_digest equals the base digest the seed copied; any other event-less head, or a seed that disagrees with its branch record, is still refused. #97 (found by the #96 seam's own row 'a candidate branch that later has events is treated as event-backed'): recovery seeded EVERY (stream, branch) chain from genesis (revision 0 / digest null), so a candidate whose first event legitimately carries streamRevision = base + 1 and previousStreamEventDigest = the base digest failed verification; a non-root branch with a branch record now starts its walk at (baseRevision, baseDigest), root / a missing record / a missing table keep the genesis start. The worker also found WHY that row threw a wrapped error before the walk ran at all: the STORED event and receipt Zod schemas still pinned branchId to the root literal (only expectedBranchId had been widened in B2), so hydrating a candidate-branch row rejected - the stored-schema pin is lifted to the same EventBranchId the contract already promised. Rows: the it.failing row is a plain passing row; an event-less head on an unknown branch, a seed at the wrong revision, a seed with the wrong digest are refused; two seeds on two candidates of one stream are admitted; a candidate with events is event-backed and its head must match its chain; a candidate whose first event chains from genesis instead of the base, or from the wrong digest, is refused; root still starts at genesis; an older database without the branch table behaves as today. Orchestrator mutants in the PR body. NOT CLAIMED: the WRITER side (#98 - verifyHead looks for the base event on the child branch while a seed's base event lives on the parent) is fixed on C1c-ii's branch, not here; recovery still refuses genuine corruption; how heads and events are written is unchanged.

  - FINDINGS #92-#93 (2026-09-02, Wave E5 cut B): #92 a cut A pin describes a body production no longer sends - campaignLifecycleState.test.ts 'reads a rebuild out of the commands route's blocked body' constructs the 409 without a recoveryAction and pins recoveryAction: null, while since B2c's #66 fix the real body carries recoveryAction: 'retry-after-rebuild'; the door reads the field generically so behaviour is right, but the fixture is less real than the route (folded inline as cut C). #93 the staleness family's recovery label overclaims: RECOVERIES.STALE_BRANCH / PROJECTION_REWOUND read 'Resync to active head' but the handler on BOTH surfaces is onClearRefusal -> setLastRefusalCode(null), which clears a local hint and lets the server answer again - no resync exists to wire it to (no client calls the commands route); honest for 'Check again', overclaiming for staleness; ruling: truthful labels derived from the handler (cut C), a real resync action is the cutover's.
  - FINDINGS #88-#90 (2026-09-02, Wave E3 PR-A): #88 a phase advance cannot commit while a player is partitioned - ending a phase needs BOTH players (#23), so a partitioned guest structurally blocks the very commit E2E-66's letter requires to happen WHILE it is partitioned; a partition scenario must pick a command one player can commit alone (GoProne, the tactical packs' command). #89 a player's wire carries NO authority sequence: the post-reconnect frame is 'Event deliverySequence=5, event.type=movement_locked, event.sequence=undefined' - the viewer projection strips it by design (the privacy pack's E2E-27 asserts precisely its absence), so an exactly-once claim on a player's wire keys on deliverySequence, with a contiguity check so a GAP fails as firmly as a repeat. #90 (found by an orchestrator LIVE mutant that SURVIVED - resumeFrom = afterLastHeld, ServerMatchHost.ts:787 - because the row's counter read only the singular top-level deliverySequence and every ReplayChunk carries a PLURAL deliverySequences[] array): a reconnect-by-RELOAD replays the viewer stream from zero, re-sending delivery numbers the client already held, while the missed event is ALSO delivered live immediately before (measured frame order: Event deliverySequence=5, ReplayStart, ReplayChunk [0..5], ReplayEnd); a reconnect-IN-PLACE takes the live outbox path and never enters the replay code at all (zero ReplayChunks) - so a session-wide 'no delivery number repeats' claim is FALSE against correct behaviour, and the letter ('apply every authorized event once from the durable viewer cursor') is APPLY-side. RULING: E2E-66 splits into R66a (in-place: one new live delivery, contiguous, zero ReplayChunks, applied once) and R66b (reload: live Event then from-zero replay, no repeat WITHIN the replay stream, contiguous coverage, the missed event in both, applied ONCE); the apply-side choke point is admitLiveEvent (client.ts:870) with admitByDelivery / admitByIdentity, and ReplayChunk routes through the SAME admission as live traffic - measured: the client does NOT double-apply the overlap (no #91 defect); the observable is what the client emits to its consumer, because the durable match log is keyed ['matchId','sequence'] (a second apply overwrites, invisible to a count) and the networked surface renders no per-event list. LAW 47: a surviving live mutant is first checked for REACHABILITY by the row's own path.
