  - Progress (2026-09-02 - FINDING #21 was a REAL LEAK on an UNAUTHENTICATED ADMIN API, fixed; FINDING #23 E2E-12 fixed at its true cause; findings #26/#27, Wave A3c): the three-context blob assertion was catching Player 1's dashboard rendering EVERY participant's principal id in the share-grant roster, each with a working Revoke button. Root cause: campaignShareService.requireSourceCampaign called evaluateSourceMutationGate, which answers 'does THIS SERVER own the campaign' (a property of the campaign - ISourceCampaignAuthority is {role:'source'} and names nobody), standing in for 'is the caller the owner', which it never enforced; and GET/POST/DELETE on /api/campaigns/[id]/grants read NO Authorization header at all. Any client that knew a campaign id could read the roster, issue itself a grant with arbitrary scopes, or revoke another participant. Fix at the boundary that should have withheld it (the route via the service, never the UI): authenticateRequest with an expectedScope DERIVED SERVER-SIDE from the campaign's own coopSession.matchId (never read off the request) before the verb switch -> 401; requireCampaignGm keyed on the active 'gm' row of campaign_session_participant (migration 17, revoked_at IS NULL) -> 403 not-campaign-gm; callerId now REQUIRED on all three service entry points; the GM's share panel presents the co-op token it already holds. Room-code guest grants go through the store, not the service - unaffected (three-context-pack + proposal-pack green). Red-first: with the gate scaffolded but unenforced an outsider with no seat received the full grant list (5/6 red). Mutants killed: GM check accepts everyone (6 red), 401 dropped, revoked filter dropped, expected scope undefined. authority-privacy-three-context.spec.ts now runs in a CI-gated group (three-context-pack:30) - it ran in NONE, which is why an unauthenticated admin API went unnoticed. FINDING #26 (NOT fixed, wider seam): the rest of the campaign REST surface - adopt, commands, replica-sync, [id], index, redeem - still reads no Authorization header; and there is still no owner principal on the campaign record (the gate keys on the co-op gm seat, so a solo campaign now gets 403 and an empty share panel - a deliberate narrowing with no practical loss since grants are only issued through co-op). FINDING #23 diagnosed by instrumenting both action bars at the timeout: NOT the poll racing the toast - a collided client SELF-PAUSES (every control disabled incl. Concede), the D4 turn-ownership gate withholds the advance control from the non-active side, and ending a phase needs BOTH players, so once anyone is wedged the phase cannot advance at all (initiative-dependent ~40% of runs; wedging the passive side and driving from the active side still failed 4/4). The row only ever passed by clicking the wedged client's control in the instant before its pause rendered. Fix removes the dependency instead of winning the race: the collision targets the FIRST delivered event of the commit (phase_changed is the LAST event of its commit, proven 4/4) so the rest of that commit IS the later delivery, and the row now asserts POSITIVELY that a later-numbered frame arrived before asserting the cursor did not follow it - strengthened, not loosened; 8 green runs. FINDING #27 (product question, flagged not answered): a collided client stalling a match for both seats. Live under the orchestrator: exactly-once 4/4 x2, three-context 1/1, proposal 1/1, privacy 5/5, authority-pack1 3/3. Environmental note: three recurring dev-server flake modes at fixture setup on this machine (PageNotFoundError for a dynamic route, waitForURL timeouts, Network.getResponseBody missing) - `rm -rf .next` cleared a persistent bout; its own seam. Acceptance ledger: E2E-03/05/06/08/09/10/11/12/13/15/16/18/20/21/22/23/24/26/27/30 live (20) across nine runner groups.

  - Progress (2026-09-02 - E2E-14 STAYS OPEN for a MEASURED reason; findings #18/#19/#21, Wave A2b): the product half of E2E-14's letter is now true (see the 7.3 errata: the bounded queue covers the fact path, a stalled viewer enters a recoverable behind state and resumes at the first missed frame), but the browser scenario cannot be closed by the e2e cap override Wave A2 proposed. FINDING #19: the override seam was built and VERIFIED to reach the socket host's module graph (sentinel file; the broadcaster's admission check logged cap=1024 after arming) and still never trips, because ws.bufferedAmount is the Node stream's PENDING-WRITE backlog, not bytes the client has not consumed - it stays 0 until the OS send buffer refuses a write (O(100 KB) in flight): 0 on all 60 admission observations through a 45-second fully blocked renderer; a 37-second ~1 KB/s CDP throttle left the stalled client never behind, never reaped, finally holding all 37 frames contiguously. A turn-limited match puts ~37 frames / ~17 KB on one socket - an order of magnitude short - so lowering the cap compares against zero, and A2's premise (their 3.9 MB figure came from pushing megabytes, not match traffic) was wrong. The seam was REMOVED rather than shipped as dead code (reversal cost: route + shell + 9 route rows + 5 cap rows; one boundary row kept against the shipped constant). What closes E2E-14 honestly: an APPLICATION-level backlog bound (sent minus acknowledged over the existing DeliveryAck stream) alongside or instead of bufferedAmount - a design change with its own plan; the 10x-volume route guesses at an unmeasured absorption floor and is not recommended. Not claimed: no typed close code on reap (ServerMatchSocketLifecycle closes with no status). FINDING #21 (pre-existing, not caused here - fails identically with the fix stashed): e2e/authority-privacy-three-context.spec.ts is RED on main on a DOM leak-sweep assertion (a pid_ identifier present in page content); it is not in any CI-gated group; owner: the privacy pack (Wave A3b to diagnose after its current seam).

  - Progress (2026-09-02 - FINDING #12 RESOLVED: one live-delivery path for every CampaignJoin arm, Wave A1): CampaignSyncSession.attachLiveParticipant is now the ONLY host subscription any join arm takes - admitToWire-scoped per recipient through the 11.1 boundary and delivery-recorded where delivery happens. The host arm no longer hand-builds a CampaignSnapshot plus a direct UNSCOPED host.subscribe: it hydrates through joinMember like every other client (the GM baseline now carries matchId + revision), and joinMember/retainHydratedParticipant refuse to retain the GM (retaining the authority gates the GM's next command on the GM's own ack - a deadlock; the two binder rows that assert launch ok with the GM connected pin it). The grant arm retains the guest at the journal head FIRST, then attaches the unified sink BEFORE its hydration reads (a commit landing mid-hydration is buffered behind the snapshot gate, never lost between 'not in the snapshot' and 'not yet subscribed'; the rejoin path flushes that buffer instead of dropping it). roomCodeGuestLiveFanout is ingest-only: the grant channel keeps the durable replica current and emits nothing to the client wire. campaignEventWireFrame is the one event->frame mapping. Acceptance harness campaignLiveDeliveryUnification.test.ts (7 socket-level rows across all three arms sharing one registry entry) was captured RED-FIRST against pristine main by a second agent working the same finding - its two red rows (GM hydration still hand-built; grant guests received live frames with zero convergence bookkeeping, so evaluateScenarioLaunch read converged while they were arbitrarily behind and their CampaignAck was refused unknown-participant) are exactly the gaps the first implementation had, and are closed. Orchestrator mutants (all killed): retention seed removed from the grant arm (1 red); GM retained by joinMember (4 red); scope admission bypassed inside attachLiveParticipant (1 red - the gm-scope-withheld cross-arm row). NAMED WIRE-SHAPE CHANGE: room-code guests' live CampaignEvent frames now carry the JOURNAL sequence rather than the grant projector's per-grant delivery sequence (the key was always present; the number space changed to match the legacy arm and the ack gate, per the sequence-concealment deferral recorded in campaignWireScopeBoundary.ts); no leakScan expectation covered that arm, a harness row pins the change instead. E2E-03 and E2E-08 are REVIVED in authority-pack1 with eventSequence captured per frame; E2E-08 has each guest ack the post-advance revision before the verbatim resend and asserts no Error frame reached the GM, so it can only pass on idempotency, never on a CAMPAIGN_NOT_CONVERGED refusal. Named residual: a retained-but-unstreamed grant guest (hydration failure after retention) stays retained until their retry re-seeds - consistent with the binder's bound-but-unstreamed-member stance. Full suite 2467/33590 green; tsc 0; oxlint 77-pin.
  - Progress (2026-09-02 - TENTH SCENARIO LIVE: E2E-15 host restart recovers every authority clause; E2E-14 BLOCKED on volume, Wave A2): E2E-15 is green in the new resilience-pack group (behind the relaunching wrapper; RESPAWNING_GROUPS now names restart-pack and resilience-pack, the qc suite pins both plus a non-respawning group's plain server). Shape: a real 1v1 vault flow to Movement plus a server-persisted campaign; process-exit-after-commit armed; the advance commits and the process dies unpublished; BOTH browser contexts go setOffline before the respawn can answer them, so the post-restart evidence window carries zero client traffic (no pending-intent retry, no rejoin replay, no ack) and the ORDERING claim is assertable exactly: server.js awaits bootstrapMultiplayerServer (match replay, cursor restore, outbox drain) before listen, so the first 200 is already downstream of recovery. Seven clauses read at that instant: campaign (id, name, version, instanceId), match status + room code, participants, receipts (count grew and coverage reaches the log head), cursors (same player set, never rewound), pending outbox (total grew, pending 0 with no client attached), authorized projections (both still hold a stream); then connectivity returns, both surfaces reach a commandsEnabled posture with no test-driven reload, and a new command round-trips. ACTIVE BRANCH is deferred, not faked: the tactical client emits neither PROJECTION_REWOUND nor PROJECTION_REBUILDING today, so the clause lands with the branches leaf. Orchestrator re-ran the pack live: 1/1 green (11.7s), [mp-boot] recovered 1 active match(es) observed after the death. Would-be mutant (reasoned, src outside the seam): deleting resumePendingEventPublications in MatchRecovery leaves E2E-05/06 green and fails E2E-15 on outboxPending 0. Honest limit: the cursor clause proves the rows survived, not that the host reloaded them - a restorePersistedViewerDeliveries deletion is NOT caught here. E2E-06's carried question (does the client's automatic reconnect outlast a respawn) STAYS OPEN and this harness cannot answer it: the e2e server runs Next in dev mode and the dev HMR client hard-reloads the document when the server returns (measured in every run: a fresh document GET on the match route right after mp-boot), so the observed path is reload-equivalent; the test RECORDS which path carried each page (reconnect-path annotation) rather than asserting a reconnect it did not witness. Settling it needs the pack behind a production build. E2E-14 (slow-client isolation) is BLOCKED and the charter's stated obstacle was wrong: a standalone ws+Chromium probe measured CDP network throttling DOES reach the server socket's bufferedAmount (4.0 MB throttled, 3.9 MB with a blocked renderer, cap 1,048,576) - the stall lever exists; what is missing is VOLUME: mean durable event 470 B, 3 events per command, so ~590 commands must queue on one stalled socket before MAX_BUFFERED_BYTES trips, more than a turn-limited match contains. Unblocking = an e2e-mode-gated override of the cap in ServerMatchBroadcaster (the fault-kind seam shape); with a small cap the throttle lever reaches it in a handful of commands. Acceptance ledger: E2E-05/06/09/10/11/12/13/15/16/18 live across six runner groups (E2E-03/08 ride Wave A1).

  - Progress (2026-08-31 - box stays OPEN; FIRST SCENARIO LANDED + FINDING #12): E2E-18 is green and honest - three real browser contexts on the campaign channel sit QUIET for the full HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_MS plus a server period (a genuine ~75s hold; the first draft's expect.poll resolved on first success in 23s and was rejected as vacuous - survival BEYOND the interval is the letter), heartbeats flowing both ways, all three still connected after; registered as runner group authority-pack1 ('authority' stays reserved for the complete pack per 21.4). MARATHON FINDING #12, instrumented server-side across six live probe runs: a committed campaign command's event reaches NO connected campaign client. The campaign socket has THREE join arms with THREE different live-delivery behaviors - the host arm subscribes a direct unscoped sendCampaignEvent; the legacy newcomer arm subscribes via the session's admitToWire-scoped sinks; the grant arm attaches snapshot-gated epoch-PROJECTION subscribers that push no raw frames - and in the live fixture an AdvanceDay commit broadcast to 3 subscribers produced exactly ONE wire send (readyState 1) that the browser never received (raw message count stayed 1), zero admitToWire evaluations, zero errors anywhere. Which arm a client lands on depends on server state (hostPlayerId match, issuedInvite, grant channel), which is precisely the Visibility-Equivalent-Across-Surfaces violation the 12.x owner fork names; E2E-03/08 (their letters and their spec scaffolding retained in the pack file) are BLOCKED on this delivery unification, not on test authoring. The instrumentation lives in the session transcripts; the probe sequence (intent-arrival -> result-ok -> subscribers=3 -> one-send -> raw=1) is reproducible via MEKSTATION_E2E_SERVER_COMMAND log capture.

- [ ] 21.2 Implement and pass E2E-19 through E2E-30 for pre-serialization privacy, sealed choices, immediate public facts, fog projections, live/replay/export parity, unauthorized access, veto/timeout, and simultaneous proposal attribution.
  - FINDING #101 (2026-09-02; CLOSED in the same PR): proposal-pack E2E-30 waited for guest-proposal-vetoed after clicking veto-<id>, but the GM review surface now opens a confirmation dialog (VetoConfirmationDialog, veto-confirm) before the decision lands - the row's drive stopped one click short. The test id is composed from the tracked status and still renders after a real veto; the row now confirms. Live alone after the fix: proposal-pack 1/1.

  - PROGRESS (2026-09-02, Wave B4 arbiter timeout, defect #22 CLOSED): the co-op GM arbiter's proposal timeout was UNARMED in production - both construction sites (CampaignHostRegistry and coopRuntimeSession) passed proposalTimeoutMs: 0, so autoVetoForTimeout ('host-review-timeout'), unit-tested since 11.x, never fired for a real guest. One exported constant PRODUCTION_PROPOSAL_TIMEOUT_MS = 120000 (campaignGmArbiterTimeout.ts; the change specs require a timeout that commits nothing - E2E-29 - but name no duration; two minutes is long enough for a GM to decide and short enough that a guest is not stuck behind an absent host; the omitted-option fallback DEFAULT_PROPOSAL_TIMEOUT_MS stays 5 min; tests may still pass 0 to disable). Rows on PRODUCTION construction, not a hand-built arbiter: R1 an undecided proposal past the production timeout is auto-vetoed with host-review-timeout and the guest is told through the existing CampaignDecision path, ledger unchanged (fake timers with Date un-faked so heartbeat idle does not reap sockets); R2 a decision before the timeout returns the timer count to baseline and advancing the interval does not un-commit; R3 disposing the registry returns the timer count to baseline (no dangling timer keeps the process alive); R4 both production sites expose the same constant (a future site cannot quietly go back to 0); plus the runtime session's result waiters resolve on timeout. Orchestrator mutants in the PR body. NOT CLAIMED: E2E-29's browser row (a separate seam); reconnect after a missed timeout frame; a real 120 s wall-clock wait.
  - NOTE (2026-09-02, Wave E7): defect #15's 'lobby passes no GM callbacks' is superseded - the two-button stub was replaced by the rewind preview flow (E6) and the lobby now passes the preview producer; the GM-command letters (E2E-19/25) still wait on the commit half (3b-iv).
  - PROGRESS (2026-09-02, Wave E8, E2E-28's HTTP + export slice): main had NO timeline or export HTTP route - 12.x delivered the service half only (ViewerHistoryService registers no routes). Two GET routes now exist, /api/matches/:id/timeline and /api/matches/:id/export, through one shared chain (matchHistoryViewerChain: rejectUnexpectedMethod GET, initializeApiDatabase, id guard, authenticateRequest with the { kind: 'match', id } scope the preview route omits, then the SERVICE call - the route never pre-brands a viewer, because every service entrypoint re-resolves the branded viewer itself for kind 'timeline' / 'export') and a production factory (AuthorizedViewerResolver over MatchSeatMembershipSource on the default match store; SQLite audit, private-record and delivery-epoch repositories on the service database; ViewerProjectionService with the match-wire and history-proof projectors). The timeline body is { timeline, timelineDigest: viewerTimelineDigest(entries) } because the service returns a bare array and the invariant needs the digest on both bodies; the export body is the service's IViewerHistoryExport verbatim (application/json, no file wrapping); transport {error} for 400 (id / streamType), 401, 403 (constant, id-free), 405 (Allow GET), 500. INVARIANT pinned: a seated viewer's export timelineDigest equals the same viewer's timeline timelineDigest equals viewerTimelineDigest(export.timeline).
